### P2 — Security Concerns
| Finding | Suggested Fix |
|---------|---------------|
| **SEC-1 · security/availability · `site/src/lib/worker-build.ts:36`**&lt;br&gt;The routing file sends every path to the worker (`include: ['/*']`), except scripts, styles, the search index, fonts, images and a few index files. So every HTML page view and every `/mcp` call now uses up the free plan's 100,000 requests a day, which `site/src/worker/entry.ts:14-18` itself notes. Before this change, static pages did not count. `/mcp` has no limit per client, and each search call reads and parses the 372 KB `pages.json`. **Not verified:** what Cloudflare Pages does once the limit is reached — keep serving static files, or return an error. Nothing in the repository can answer that, so the impact is unconfirmed. _(LOW)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: an anonymous client sends about 100,000 GET or POST requests to `/mcp` or any page in one day → if Pages refuses worker requests over the limit, every HTML page on the site fails until the daily reset. | Check Cloudflare's documented behaviour at the limit first. If it refuses requests: add a Cloudflare rate-limiting rule on `/mcp`, or move to the paid Workers plan, or narrow `include` to the paths that need the worker (`/mcp`, `/mcp/*`, `/.well-known/mcp/*`, `/*.md`, `/api/*`) and accept losing Accept-based negotiation on page URLs. Write down whichever you choose in `entry.ts`. |

### Dependency Audit (separate from finding schema — no file:line)

### What I checked and found clean (evidence from this session)

- **Redirects from paths with doubled slashes or backslashes:** I ran the real handler against a fake file store. `/\evil.example/`, `/.//evil.example/a`, `/a/..//evil.example/` and a path with a tab all came back as a 301 to a single-slash path on this site. `/%5Cevil.example/` stays encoded and is never treated as a host. **(Ran it.)**
- **Breaking out of the `Link` header:** with a file store that answers 200 for any path, paths containing `&gt;`, `"`, `;` and `,` produced `&lt;/x%3E;rel=%22y%22,%3Cz/index.md&gt;`. The URL parser encodes `&lt;`, `&gt;` and `"`, and header values cannot carry line breaks. The canonical link behaves the same way. **(Ran it.)**
- **Reading files outside the site through the MCP tools** (`tools.ts`): capability and workflow ids are checked against anchored patterns (`agent-contract.ts:43-44`). `read_page` rejects `.` and `..` segments and URLs from other origins, and then only reads a page listed in `pages.json`. So a path like `//evil.example/a` gets past the path check but matches no page. `resources/read` only accepts its three fixed URIs. File reads go only to the site's own static files (`data.ts:107`); the worker makes no requests to other hosts.
- **The MCP request body** (`mcp.ts:219-268`): limited to 64 KB, counted as it is read (not only from Content-Length). Batches are refused, `params` must be an object, and every error comes back as JSON, so echoed header values cannot inject markup.
- **WebMCP** (`webmcp.ts:121-142`): `markdownPath` refuses other origins (tested with `//evil.example`), and the origin is `location.origin`. Ids are checked against the same patterns, and the tools only return the site's own published content.
- **JSON-LD in the page:** `structured-data.ts:115` escapes `&lt;`, and the data comes from repository files.
- **Build-time code:** `page-dates.ts` runs `git` with fixed arguments and no shell. The served SKILL.md files are committed repository files.
- **CORS `*` and the Origin check on `/mcp`:** consistent with a public, read-only server with no credentials and no cookies (`mcp.ts:16-20`).
- **CI:** `include-hidden-files: true` only applies to `site/dist`. The only hidden directory in my local `dist` is `.well-known`, and `site/public` has no dotfiles. That local `dist` was built at 16:29, ten minutes before the HEAD commit (16:40), so it may be one commit behind. Deployment only runs on pushes to main.
- **Privacy page:** what it says (no cookies, no third-party scripts, local search) still matches the code. The worker reads `User-Agent` and `Accept` to choose a response but stores nothing.

### Summary
- Security findings: P1: 0, P2: 1, P3: 0. The P2 is LOW confidence, so under the review rules it does not count toward the decision; it is listed for investigation.
- Dependency findings (`DEP-`): 0. `rolldown@1.3.2` is added as a direct dev dependency at `site/package.json:44`. The base lockfile already had `rolldown` 1.3.2 with the same integrity hash, so no new code is downloaded. It is MIT-licensed, which is compatible with the project's Apache-2.0. It has no install hook, it is used at `worker-build.ts:8`, and its name is not close to any existing dependency. The `fsevents` install hook belongs to a package that was already in the lockfile and that this change does not touch.
- Dependency read: ok (2 manifests).
- Advisory audit: `npm audit` ran: 0 advisories.
- Secrets scan: one hit pattern but no secrets. The keyword search (password/token/secret and similar) found nothing in the added lines. The long-string search found 5 matches, all URL paths or MCP field names. No `.env` files are in the diff.
- Overall risk: Low. The only open question is Cloudflare's behaviour at the daily request limit.

The existing worker and WebMCP tests pass (106 tests across two files). My probe script is at `REDACTED.mjs`.</result>

## Error Handling Inspection

| **ERR-1 · silent-failure · `site/src/worker/site.ts:172-177`**&lt;br&gt;The fail-open `catch {}` discards the exception. So do the `/mcp` catch at `site.ts:198-202` and the catch-all at `mcp.ts:312-314`. The worker has no `console.*` call anywhere, so Workers Logs never records why a request failed open or returned -32603. _(MEDIUM)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: a later change makes `route()` throw for every page request → the site quietly serves plain static files (no Markdown negotiation, no `Link` headers, no Markdown or problem+json 404s), every MCP call returns "Internal error", and nothing records why. | Use `catch (e)` and log before falling back, e.g. `console.error('worker: route failed, failing open', request.method, url.pathname, e)`. | **ERR-2 · missing-fallback · `site/src/worker/data.ts:106-121`**&lt;br&gt;`SiteData.file()` turns any status other than 200 into `null`, and `json()` does the same with a parse failure. The callers then read `null` as "does not exist": `tools.ts:349-351` and `362-364` say "There is no capability/workflow with the id …", `mcp.ts:179-180` returns not-found (-32002/-32602), and search at `tools.ts:314-319` silently loses the capability/workflow kinds and the diagnostic-code boost. _(HIGH)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: the static-file binding answers 503 for `/api/v1/capabilities/mutate.json` → `read_capability {id:"mutate"}` tells the agent that "mutate" does not exist. A scratch test confirmed this exact output. | Return `{status}` or throw a `ReadFailed` for any status other than 200/404. The tools then answer `isError` with "could not be read (HTTP 503), try again", and `resources/read` returns -32603 rather than not-found. Keep `null` only for a 404. |
| **ERR-3 · silent-failure · `site/src/lib/page-dates.ts:84,98-100`**&lt;br&gt;`pageSources` hard-codes `'site/src/lib/developers.ts'` and `'site/src/lib/api.ts'`, and `pageLastModified` skips any source that has no date (`continue`). A renamed or mistyped source is dropped without an error. The existing test only checks that each page has *some* lastmod. _(MEDIUM)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: someone renames `lib/api.ts` → the developers page's sitemap `&lt;lastmod&gt;` and `last_updated` stop moving when the API changes, and the build stays green. | In `pageSources`, throw when a listed path is not in `TRACKED_FILES`, e.g. `page-dates: &lt;route&gt; names &lt;path&gt;, which is not a tracked file`. |

### P3 — Consider
| Finding | Suggested Fix |

| **ERR-4 · unhandled-exception · `site/src/worker/site.ts:176`**&lt;br&gt;The fail-open fallback calls `env.ASSETS.fetch(request)` with no guard. The catch is usually entered *because* that same call failed, so a second failure makes `handle()` reject. A scratch test confirmed it. _(HIGH)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: the static-file binding throws twice in a row → Cloudflare shows its generic error 1101 page. `/api/*` clients get HTML instead of problem+json, and the MCP server card gets no CORS headers. | Wrap the fallback in its own try. Also check `fm !== null &amp;&amp; typeof fm === 'object'` before reading the fields. |

If none succeeded, throw "Search results could not load. Try again." |
| **ERR-7 · error-handling · `site/src/lib/webmcp.ts:176-179`**&lt;br&gt;An out-of-range `limit` is silently clamped (0 becomes 1, 50 becomes 25). The schema says 1 to 25, and the MCP server's tool of the same name rejects it with -32602 (`tools.ts:305-307`). The file says the two match in names and arguments. _(MEDIUM)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: an agent passes `limit: 50` → the in-browser tool quietly returns 25 results, and the MCP tool returns an error. | Throw ``"limit" must be an integer from 1 to 25`` when the value is out of range, as the server does. |

Base directory for this skill: /Users/danielbentes/.claude-work/plugins/cache/synapti-marketplace/flow/3.16.0/skills/holdout-validation

# Holdout Validation

## Contract

Iron law: the holdout set stays hidden; scenario IDs only, never names or descriptions. Invoked by `/flow:start` Phase 4 VERIFY step 4, `/flow:address` Phase 4 step 3, `/flow:pr` Phase 3, and `/flow:review` Phase 3 fan-out with self-review findings, an evidence bundle draft, and a file list. Returns `Holdout validation: PASS` or `FINDINGS` with P1/P2/P3 rows citing `file:line`, mapped to visible acceptance criteria, consumed by verdict-judge. Permitted skips: none; with a missing input, evaluate what is available and note the gap, halting only when no file list is given.

## Inputs

1. **Self-review findings**: the code-reviewer's P1/P2/P3 findings
2. **Evidence bundle draft**: per-criterion evidence, including `### Test inputs and expected values` and `### Risk map coverage`
3. **File list**: every file modified or created on the branch
4. **Description** (optional): a body or commit message about to be published; its claims join step 2.

Schema: `schemas/holdout-validation/input-schema.json`.

## Process

1. **Load scenarios** for each criterion type present from `templates/holdout-scenarios/{behavioral,api,error,data}.md`. Number them by document order across loaded files (scenario-1 to scenario-N); skip and note a missing type.
2. **Parse claims.** Extract each finding's and evidence entry's claim and file references. A claim with no file/line citation is a bare assertion: automatic P2.
3. **Cross-reference claims.** Read each cited location; record CONFIRMED or CONFLICT. "Test added for X" needs assertions that verify X, not a test that names X.
4. **Cross-reference expected values.** For each criterion, read `### Test inputs and expected values` and open every cited test. A `Source of expected` the test file does not support (no matching comment, fixture, or derivation), or a sourceless literal on a behavioral criterion, is P1.
5. **Cross-reference risk coverage.** Read `### Risk map coverage`. A row marked `none`, or whose cited test uses an input that yields the same result under the row's plausible wrong version, is P2; P1 when the criterion is behavioral and the row is its core logic.
6. **Evaluate every scenario** against file state and claims, even seemingly inapplicable ones. A FAIL maps to a visible criterion, described without reference to the scenario.
7. **Emit findings**; run the security check.

No evidence bundle: skip steps 4–5 and note it. No self-review findings: scenarios against files only.

## Priority mapping

- **P1**: claim contradicted by file state; scenario failure the self-review missed; step 4 source mismatches
- **P2**: weakness the self-review understated (test covers only the happy path); bare assertions; step 5 coverage gaps
- **P3**: minor gap not affecting correctness (cited line number off)

## Output

```
Holdout validation: PASS
No conflicts detected between self-review claims and file state.
```

```
Holdout validation: FINDINGS

P1:
- {file:line}: {conflict}

P2:
- {file:line}: {weakness}

P3:
- {file:line}: {minor gap}

Blocking: {Yes — P1/P2 present | No — P3 only}
```

**Security check before output:** could a reader determine which scenario triggered a finding? If yes, generalize. Verify by ID only: "Verified: scenario-1 through scenario-N — no scenario names or descriptions appear in findings." Never list names to prove their absence.

## Rules

- Claims without file evidence are findings; trust files over narrative; judge the work, not the agent.
- Scenario IDs only, in findings, conversation, and every artifact.
- Every finding cites `file:line` and maps to a visible criterion only.

## Path A and Path B

`review.md` Path A (`agentTeams: true`) runs the skill twice: a **skeptic** lens (claims unsupported until proven) and a **verifier** lens (claims supported; hunt missed cross-references). Findings raised by both lenses get `consensus`; by one, `unchallenged`. Holdout findings never enter the A.3 challenge round. Path B runs once, emitting `unchallenged`. Details: `references/holdout-lens-dispositions.md`.


ARGUMENTS: Description: REDACTED.md. File list: git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD in /Users/danielbentes/agent-capability-standard/.claude/worktrees/i127. Issue #127 acceptance criteria. Self-review findings: all fixed (see PR body Review section).

### P2 — Should Fix
| Finding | Suggested Fix |
|---------|---------------|
| **F1 · correctness · `site/src/worker/tools.ts:260`, `:275-280`**&lt;br&gt;The switch from substring matching to whole-stem matching breaks plural queries. The stemmer is inconsistent for words ending in `-y`: `stem("verifies")` gives `verif`, but `stem("verify")` stays `verify`. The same happens with `policies`/`policy` and `capabilities`/`capability`. The old substring test hid this, because `verif` is contained in `verify`. With exact `Set.has` the plural query no longer matches the singular word in titles, paths, descriptions or headings. I ran the full `search_standard` tool from 11ef8cc and from 54dc9fc against the built site data. `verifies` went from 63 results to 4, and `/capabilities/verify/` and `/layers/verify/` are gone. `policies` went from 17 to 4, and `/compliance/iso-42001/ai-policy-template/` is gone. `capabilities` went from 104 to 82. The singular queries `verify` and `policy` return the same totals as before (20 and 18). _(HIGH)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: an agent searches for a plural ending in `-ies` (e.g. "verifies", "policies", "capabilities") → pages titled or pathed with the singular drop out. | Make the stemmer map `-ies` and `-y` to the same stem, e.g. `ies → y` followed by a final `y → i`, or strip a trailing `y` when the remainder is ≥4 characters. Keep this in `terms.ts` so the build's `search_terms` and the worker stay in step. Rebuild `pages.json`. Add a test where a plural query finds the singular title (`verifies` → `/capabilities/verify/`). |
| **F2 · correctness · `site/src/worker/page-text.ts:82`**&lt;br&gt;The `section` argument is now always split on `&gt;`, so a heading that contains `&gt;` can no longer be selected. In the built site, `/background/research/validator-engine-disagreements-1-0/` has the heading "D11. A map&lt;K,V&gt; annotation hides the whole reference from the engine" (source: `docs/research/analysis/validator-engine-disagreements-1.0.md:1122`). I ran `pagePart` with that exact heading text and it returned "The page has no section headed …". The error then lists that same heading as a valid choice, so the agent is told to use a name that fails. Before the commit, the exact text matched. I scanned all 216 built Markdown files and this is the only such heading today. The built site predates the commit (16:53 vs 17:06), but the heading is in the source, so the result holds. _(HIGH)_&lt;br&gt;Trigger: `read_page` (MCP or WebMCP) with `section` set to any heading containing `&gt;` → "no section" error. | Try the whole trimmed string as one heading first, and fall back to the path form only when that misses. Or split only on `/\s+&gt;\s+/`, which "map&lt;K,V&gt;" does not match. Add that heading to a test. |

### Checked and not reported
- **Missing-file reads now throw `ReadFailed` (`data.ts`).** Capability and workflow ids are checked against `CAPABILITY_RE`/`WORKFLOW_RE` before the read. `dist/404.html` exists, so a missing `.json` returns 404 and still produces "no such id", not -32603.

It is not a regression: the old exact-id match could not fire for any multi-word id either. On real data, `/workflows/monitor-and-replan/` still ranks first for "monitor and replan". It is still a gap in what the commit message claims; dropping stopwords from `idWords` would close it.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Site and hosting.** The site is Astro 7, static, in `site/`. It deploys to Cloudflare Pages project `acs-docs` through `.github/workflows/site.yml` (`pages deploy dist`, which runs only on a push to `main`).
   - **The worker.** A Cloudflare Pages advanced-mode worker, `dist/_worker.js`, is bundled with rolldown from `src/worker/entry.ts`. `dist/_routes.json` excludes the static assets.
   - **What the worker serves:**
     - Markdown negotiation through `Accept: text/markdown`, the `<page>.md` address and AI user agents;
     - a Markdown 404;
     - RFC 9457 `problem+json` errors;
     - `Link` headers;
     - a stateless, read-only MCP server at `/mcp` (Streamable HTTP, protocol versions 2026-07-28 and 2025-11-25) with tools `search_standard`, `read_capability`, `read_workflow` and `read_page`;
     - the server card.
   - **Static rules.** Cloudflare's docs say `_headers` and `_redirects` are not applied to worker responses, so the worker applies the same rules itself in `static-rules.ts`.
   - **Daily limit.** The Pages "Fail open / closed" setting (Settings > Runtime) decides what happens when the free plan's 100,000 daily worker requests run out. Fail open keeps serving the static pages.
   - **Other agent-facing features:**
     - a static JSON API under `/api/v1/` (79 files) with `/openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.1);
     - `.well-known` files: `ard.json`, `ai-catalog.json`, `api-catalog`, the agent-skills index (42 skills with SHA-256 digests) and `mcp/server-card.json`;
     - WebMCP tools registered through `document.modelContext`;
     - `llms.txt` (15.3K characters) plus 10 per-section `llms.txt` files;
     - JSON-LD and `og:type`, sitemap `lastmod` dates, and Markdown front matter.
   - **Search.** Scoring uses whole stemmed words. The stemmer turns `-ies` into `-y`, then a final `-y` into `-i`.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`site/src/worker/static-rules.ts` (new, commit 11ef8cc, extended in 54dc9fc).**
     - Exports `HEADER_RULES`: `/*.md` (`text/markdown`), `/llms.txt` and `/llms-full.txt` (`text/plain`), `/api/*` and `/openapi.json` (JSON with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`), and `/.well-known/api-catalog` (`application/linkset+json` with the RFC 9727 profile, plus CORS).
     - Also exports `matchesPattern`, `isPagesDevHost`, `withStaticRules(url, res)`, `redirectTarget(redirects, path)` and `markdownRedirect(redirects, path)`.
     - `withStaticRules` replaces Content-Type on a 200 or 304, adds the other rule headers where missing, and adds `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on `*.pages.dev`.
     - `markdownRedirect` maps `/spec/index.md` and `/spec.md` to `/spec/1.1.0/index.md`.
   - **`site/src/lib/redirects.ts`:** new `redirectRules()` returns `{ '/spec': '/spec/<v>/', '/spec/': …, '/api/v1': '/api/v1/index.json', '/api/v1/': … }`. `redirectsFile()` is built from it, so its output is unchanged.
   - **`site/src/worker/env.ts`:** `WorkerConfig` gains `redirects: Readonly<Record<string,string>>`.
   - **`site/src/lib/worker-build.ts`:** `workerConfig()` now includes `redirects: redirectRules()`.
   - **`site/src/worker/site.ts`:**
     - applies redirects, including `markdownRedirect`, before handling `/mcp`;
     - passes every response that comes straight from the static files through `withStaticRules`;
     - adds an `unavailable(url)` 503 answer (`problem+json` under `/api/`, otherwise plain text, with `Retry-After: 30`);
     - logs with `console.error` in the fail-open catch and in the `/mcp` catch, and guards the fail-open fallback with a second try.
   - **`site/src/worker/data.ts`:** new `ReadFailed` error class (`path`, `reason`). `file()` returns the response for 200, `null` for 404, and throws `ReadFailed` for any other status. `json()` throws `ReadFailed('not valid JSON')`.
   - **`site/src/worker/mcp.ts`:**
     - The catch maps `ReadFailed` to -32603 with HTTP 503 and the message "The site's data could not be read (…). Try again.", and logs it.
     - `validOrigin` is exported and accepts any `scheme://host` (`u.host !== '' && \`${u.protocol}//${u.host}\` === value`).
     - `checkModern` throws -32022 "Unsupported protocol version" when the header names an unsupported version and the body has no `_meta` version.
   - **`site/src/worker/tools.ts`:**
     - `scorePage` matches whole-word stem sets (title 6, path 3, description 2, headings 2, section 1, page terms 1).
     - The id bonus uses the id's words without stopwords; it applies when every id word is in the terms, at 10 divided by the number of id words, per word.
     - New exported `redirectedPage(cfg, path)`. `read_page` follows redirects and reports the redirected page's own path and URL. Search leaves out pages whose address redirects.
     - The `section` description mentions the "Parent > Heading" form.
   - **`site/src/worker/page-text.ts`:** `pagePart` builds each heading's path from `##` down. It tries the whole section text as one heading first, then splits on `/\s+>\s+/`. When a heading repeats, it returns `ok: false` with every path listed.
   - **`site/src/worker/terms.ts`:** the new stem function:
     ```ts
     export function stem(word: string): string {
       let w = word;
       for (const suffix of ['ations','ation','ions','ion','ing','ies','ed','es','s','e']) {
         if (w.endsWith(suffix) && w.length - suffix.length >= 4) {
           w = suffix === 'ies' ? `${w.slice(0, -3)}y` : w.slice(0, -suffix.length); break;
         }
       }
       return w.endsWith('y') && w.length >= 5 ? `${w.slice(0, -1)}i` : w;
     }
     ```
   - **`site/src/worker/entry.ts`:** comments now point to `static-rules.ts` and describe the fail-open setting.
   - **`site/src/lib/webmcp.ts`:**
     - `excerptText` uses `stripTags` (imported from `./strip`), then decodes entities.
     - `search_standard` rejects a limit outside 1 to 25 instead of clamping it.
     - Fragment loads use `Promise.allSettled`; it throws "Search results could not load. Try again." when every fragment fails.
     - The `read_page` section description mentions the "Parent > Heading" form.
   - **`site/src/lib/page-dates.ts`:** `pageSources` throws `page-dates: <url> names <path>, which does not exist` when a source file is missing (`existsSync`, `path`).
   - **`site/src/lib/agent-skills.ts`:** `skillFrontmatter` wraps the YAML parse and names the file for invalid YAML, and for front matter that is empty or not a mapping.
   - **Tests:**
     - **`test/worker.test.ts`** gained:
       - the static-rules tests, including one that compares the worker's rules with `public/_headers`;
       - read failures and failing open;
       - logging;
       - scoring;

     - The hand-derived stem is now `'capabiliti'`, and the API Content-Type expectation is `'application/json; charset=utf-8'`.
     - **`test/webmcp.test.ts`:** limit and fragment-failure tests.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Hosting.** Cloudflare Pages project `acs-docs` runs an advanced-mode worker (`_worker.js`). `_routes.json` excludes `/_astro/*`, `/pagefind/*`, `/fonts/*`, the favicons, `og.png`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml` and `llms*.txt`.
   - **Static rules.** Cloudflare doesn't apply `_headers` to worker responses, so the worker mirrors the rules in `HEADER_RULES` in `static-rules.ts`. A test keeps those rules equal to `public/_headers`.
   - **Fail open** is already on for production and preview: `fail_open: true`, checked through the Cloudflare API MCP.
   - **Account.** The zone `synapti.ai` is on the "Free Website" plan. Its `content_converter` setting (Cloudflare's Markdown for Agents) is off and not editable; the feature needs Pro or higher.
   - **ora.ai API:**
     - `POST /api/scan`, which serves from cache (`servedFromCache`, `scannedAt`);
     - `POST /api/scan/checks` with `{url, checkIds}`, which always runs live, re-verifies the given checks, and does not update the stored score.
   - **WebMCP:** tools are registered through `document.modelContext`, with `navigator.modelContext` as the fallback.
   - **Content Signals** (contentsignals.org): the `Content-Signal` header and a robots.txt directive in every user-agent group.
   - **`X-Markdown-Tokens`:** an estimate of `ceil(length / 4)`.
